Align `Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream HEAD per the triage CSV. (4) Update stale docs identified in Sprint 1's prep and the plan's §2g: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/README.md:39`, `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md:22`, `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md:70`, and `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md:202`. Remove "vendored subtree" / "DO NOT MODIFY" wording referring to Wan2GP/ and replace with submodule-accurate language. NON-NEGOTIABLE: Sprint 2 does NOT touch any files under `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` (that's Sprint 3's hot zone). Sprint 2 does NOT change any behavior — only relocates the vendored tree under submodule management and aligns low-risk drift. Verification gates that MUST pass before Sprint 2 is declared done: (A) strict path-contract smoke: run `python3 -c "import os, sys; from source.core.runtime_paths import ensure_wan2gp_on_path, get_wan2gp_path; ensure_wan2gp_on_path(); assert os.path.abspath(str(get_wan2gp_path())) in [os.path.abspath(p) for p in sys.path]; assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_handler.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py'); print('path-contract smoke OK')"` exits 0. (B) Full 6-file travel test suite green: `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py`. (C) Doc-sweep gate: `rg "vendored.*subtree" docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md` returns zero hits; `rg -l 'Wan2GP' docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md | xargs -I{} rg -H -n 'DO NOT MODIFY' {}` returns zero hits. All three gates are hard requirements. Rollback plan if any gate fails: `git submodule deinit Wan2GP && git reset --hard wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline`. Authentication: `gh auth status` confirmed peteromallet with repo+read:org scopes; banodoco/Wan2GP fork from Sprint 1 is accessible. Test environment: `/tmp/audit-venv/bin/python -m pytest ...` is a known-good venv with pytest 9.0.3 on Python 3.10.18; `uv run --python 3.10` is the canonical per the plan but the audit venv may be faster for verification.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 2 (Revised v3)

## Overview

Sprint 2 replaces `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` (a 1400-file flat-copied tree, currently tracked in reigh-worker git) with a git submodule mounted at the same path, pointing at an integration branch on `banodoco/Wan2GP`. Four concrete outputs:

1. Submodule cutover that preserves the SD-001 path contract. Keeping `Wan2GP/` on `sys.path` resolves **all** bare-module imports across the vendored runtime — the three named in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py:76-88` (`models.qwen.qwen_handler`, `shared.utils.loras_mutipliers`, `models.qwen.qwen_main`) plus every other tree-wide bare caller (`Wan2GP/wgp.py`, `Wan2GP/models/wan/any2video.py`, `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/*.py`, preprocessors, plugins). Same seam covers all of them.
2. `shared/` + `postprocessing/` resync honoring `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` bucket decisions.
3. `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py` introduced from upstream HEAD plus its companion preset `Wan2GP/profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json` (both bucket-(a) per CSV rows 110 and 195). SD-005 off-by-default is satisfied because no `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2*.json` is modified to reference `res_2s` — preset is opt-in only. `Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` aligned to upstream HEAD.
4. Stale-doc wording removed from `README.md:39`, `STRUCTURE.md:22`, `STRUCTURE.md:70`, `docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md:202`, and from `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` wherever the hard `rg` gate patterns match. Replacement text must itself pass the gates (no self-matching).

**LTX2 non-negotiable clarified**: The rule applies to reigh-worker-visible bytes in `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/`. Verification uses a **per-file content manifest** captured before cutover and compared after cutover. A `git diff` across the submodule boundary does NOT work (the outer repo tracks only a gitlink post-cutover); the manifest approach works regardless of tree-vs-submodule shape. See Steps 1.3 and 6.3.

**Doc-sweep gate clarified**: Both `rg` commands from the idea must return **zero hits**, full stop. No file excluded. Any replacement text written into `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` must itself contain neither `vendored` co-occurring with `subtree` on any line, nor the uppercase non-modification notice anywhere.

**Fork-repo path namespace**: The scratch fork at `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/` uses paths relative to the fork root (`shared/`, `postprocessing/`, `models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`, `profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json`). The CSV paths carry the `Wan2GP/` prefix — strip it when issuing `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork ...` commands and when asserting fork-side diff scope.

## Phase 0: Preflight

### Step 1: Record baseline tag and capture pre-cutover ltx2 manifest (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** working tree is clean (expected untracked only: `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`, `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`, `scripts/live_test/`). Confirm HEAD is current `main`.
2. **Create annotated tag** `wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline` on reigh-worker HEAD: `git tag -a wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline -m "Sprint 2 baseline"`. Do NOT push.
3. **Capture pre-cutover ltx2 per-file content fingerprint** (works identically pre- and post-cutover; survives the submodule boundary):
   ```bash
   (cd Wan2GP && find models/ltx2 -type f -not -name 'res2s.py' | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum) > /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.manifest
   sha256sum /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.manifest > /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.sha256
   ```
   The `-not -name 'res2s.py'` filter is a no-op pre-cutover (file doesn't exist yet) but ensures the post-cutover manifest (which will contain `res2s.py`) is comparable apples-to-apples.
4. **Verify fork access**: confirm `banodoco/Wan2GP` is reachable, capture `main` tip SHA + upstream `deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP` HEAD SHA.

## Phase 1: Construct the `reigh-sprint-2` fork branch

### Step 2: Clone the banodoco fork into a scratch directory (`/tmp/wan2gp-fork/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Clone**: `git clone https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git /tmp/wan2gp-fork` and `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork fetch origin main`.
2. **Branch from `main`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout -b reigh-sprint-2 origin/main`. Starting point = upstream HEAD (Sprint 1 state).
3. **Confirm** tip SHA matches Sprint 1's fork cut.

### Step 3: Mirror banodoco-drift onto `reigh-sprint-2` (byte-preserving carry commit)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Intent**: the fork branch's tree must equal `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` as-of baseline for every path — preserves all banodoco drift including the `models/ltx2/` hot zone. Bytes only, no transformation.
2. **Non-negotiable reconciliation**: the fork's carry commit writes bytes for `models/ltx2/*` on the fork, but those bytes are byte-identical to the pre-Sprint-2 reigh-worker baseline. The Sprint 2 no-touch rule governs reigh-worker bytes; Step 6.3 proves the reigh-worker-visible ltx2 tree is unchanged.
3. **Rsync copy**: from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` into `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/`, excluding `.git`, `__pycache__`, and anything already gitignored. Preserve the fork's existing `.gitignore` from upstream (don't overwrite).
4. **Stage + inspect**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork add -A` then `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork status`. Spot-check the staged delta matches expected banodoco drift: ~22 `models/ltx2/` file diffs, banodoco-only files in `defaults/` / `loras_qwen/` / `plugins/`, etc.
5. **Commit**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork commit -m "banodoco carry: mirror reigh-worker/Wan2GP baseline onto fork"`.

### Step 4: Apply Sprint 2 resync delta on top of the carry commit
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`shared/` triage application** — CSV paths are `Wan2GP/shared/<file>`; fork-repo paths are `shared/<file>` (strip the `Wan2GP/` prefix for the `git checkout` arg). For every row in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` with CSV path under `Wan2GP/shared/`:
   - **Bucket (a) `accept-upstream`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- shared/<stripped-path>`. Revert to upstream.
   - **Bucket (b) `upstreamable`**: leave as carry commit (banodoco content preserved).
   - **Bucket (c) `banodoco-fork-or-banodoco-patch`**: leave as carry commit.
   - **Bucket (d) `cruft`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork rm shared/<stripped-path>`. (None expected in `shared/`; verify by grepping CSV.)
2. **`postprocessing/` triage application**: the CSV has zero `Wan2GP/postprocessing/` rows, which means Sprint 1 triage confirmed no drift vs upstream. Verify: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork diff origin/main -- postprocessing/` must be empty. **If drift is found, HALT Sprint 2** and extend the triage CSV with proper bucket classifications before proceeding. Do NOT auto-classify.
3. **`shared/utils/self_refiner.py` alignment**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- shared/utils/self_refiner.py`. Per plan §2c, drift-only resync to upstream HEAD.
4. **`models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py` introduction**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`. CSV row 110 — bucket (a), upstream-only addition. Net-new file; no existing ltx2 file modified.
5. **`profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json` introduction**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- "profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json"`. CSV row 195 — bucket (a), upstream-only addition, paired companion to `res2s.py`. **SD-005 verification**: before committing, grep upstream's `defaults/ltx2*.json` for any `res_2s` references — expected: none. If a default config already references `res_2s`, **HALT** and re-plan (would violate SD-005).
6. **Commit the Sprint 2 delta**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork commit -m "sprint-2: resync shared/ + postprocessing/, align self_refiner, introduce res2s + HQ preset"`. Verify with `git show --stat HEAD` that the diff is confined to fork-repo paths `shared/`, `postprocessing/`, `models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`, and `profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json` (no `Wan2GP/` prefix — these are relative to `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/`).
7. **Pause for user confirmation before pushing** `reigh-sprint-2` to `banodoco/Wan2GP` (visible-to-banodoco action). After confirmation: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork push -u origin reigh-sprint-2`.
8. **Capture tip SHA** for the submodule pointer.

## Phase 2: Reigh-worker submodule cutover

### Step 5: Replace flat `Wan2GP/` tree with submodule (`reigh-worker/.gitmodules`, `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Remove tracked flat tree**: `git rm -r --cached Wan2GP/` (removes from index, leaves working copy). Then `rm -rf Wan2GP/` to remove the physical directory.
2. **Add submodule**: `git submodule add -b reigh-sprint-2 https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git Wan2GP`. Creates `.gitmodules`, populates `.git/modules/Wan2GP/`, checks out branch tip into `Wan2GP/`.
3. **Pin to the specific SHA** from Step 4.8: `git -C Wan2GP checkout <sha>` then `git add Wan2GP .gitmodules`.
4. **Verify `.gitmodules`**:
   ```
   [submodule "Wan2GP"]
       path = Wan2GP
       url = https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git
       branch = reigh-sprint-2
   ```
5. **Verify submodule health**: `git submodule status` shows `Wan2GP` at the pinned SHA with no `+`/`-` prefix.
6. **Broader caller note**: no changes required to `source/core/runtime_paths.py` — absolute `Wan2GP/` on `sys.path` continues to satisfy every bare-import caller across the vendored tree (the 3 named in `vendor_imports.py:76-88` plus all internal callers in `Wan2GP/wgp.py`, `Wan2GP/models/wan/*`, `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/*`, preprocessors, plugins). The 26+ dotted `Wan2GP.*` callers remain supported because the repo root is also on `sys.path`.
7. **Do not commit yet** — run validation gates first.

### Step 6: Validate path-contract, bridge imports, and ltx2 byte preservation (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Strict path-contract one-liner** (filesystem-only per plan §2i; safe in any Python env):
   ```bash
   python3 -c "import os, sys; from source.core.runtime_paths import ensure_wan2gp_on_path, get_wan2gp_path; ensure_wan2gp_on_path(); assert os.path.abspath(str(get_wan2gp_path())) in [os.path.abspath(p) for p in sys.path]; assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_handler.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py'); print('path-contract smoke OK')"
   ```
   Must exit 0 with `path-contract smoke OK`.
2. **Real unmocked bridge-import smoke** (runs in the worker venv where SciPy is available):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 python -c "from source.runtime.wgp_ports.vendor_imports import get_qwen_family_handler, get_shared_lora_utils_module, get_qwen_main_module; get_qwen_family_handler(); get_shared_lora_utils_module(); get_qwen_main_module(); print('bridge imports OK')"
   ```
   Real filesystem-backed exercise of the three bare-module callers. This is the direct coverage of the bridge seam.
3. **LTX2 byte-preservation check** using the manifest captured in Step 1.3 (works across the submodule boundary):
   ```bash
   (cd Wan2GP && find models/ltx2 -type f -not -name 'res2s.py' | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum) > /tmp/ltx2_post_cutover.manifest
   diff /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.manifest /tmp/ltx2_post_cutover.manifest
   ```
   `diff` must exit 0 with empty output — proves every pre-existing file under `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` has byte-identical content post-cutover. The `res2s.py` new file is excluded from both manifests so it doesn't interfere. Additionally verify `res2s.py` exists: `test -f Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`.

### Step 7: Run the 6-file travel regression suite + bridge-contract regressions (`tests/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **6-file travel suite** (idea gate B):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py
   ```
   All green. These tests don't directly exercise `vendor_imports` or `wgp_bridge` (confirmed by greps); the bridge seam is covered separately by Step 6.2's unmocked smoke. The travel suite is the Sprint 2 regression baseline for behavior preservation.
2. **Supplementary bridge-contract regressions** (covers the API surface of the bridge):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_bridge_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_registry_readonly_contracts.py
   ```
   Must be all green. Even though these tests partially monkeypatch `ensure_wan2gp_on_path`, running them post-cutover verifies the bridge API hasn't regressed at the interface level; Step 6.2 separately verifies the unmocked filesystem mount.
3. **On failure**: inspect whether a resynced `shared/` file touches travel-path code. If yes, reclassify the triggering file to bucket (c) on the fork branch, amend Step 4.1, re-push, re-pin. Do NOT silently widen the resync.

## Phase 3: Doc updates

### Step 8: Rewrite stale vendored wording (`reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`README.md:39`** — replace "vendored upstream input ... subtree" wording with submodule-accurate language describing pointer bumps and mirroring upstream requirement changes into root worker metadata before regenerating `uv.lock`.
2. **`README.md:103`** (Powered By section, if present) — correct "the `Wan2GP/` directory contains the upstream engine" to note the submodule pinned to `banodoco/Wan2GP`.
3. **`STRUCTURE.md:22`** — replace `vendored upstream. Dependency drift ...` with submodule-accurate language describing pointer bumps rather than subtree edits.
4. **`STRUCTURE.md:70`** — replace `Upstream video generation engine (vendored, DO NOT MODIFY)` with `Git submodule pointing at banodoco/Wan2GP; edit via fork PR + pointer bump, not in-place`.
5. **`docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md:202`** — replace `still a vendored upstream subtree` with submodule language; preserve the paragraph's existing guidance about mirroring requirements into root worker metadata.
6. **`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`** — required rewrites so both hard `rg` gates pass. After all edits, the file must contain zero matches for the narrow regex pattern and zero matches for the uppercase non-modification notice:
   - **Line 45**: change `as a vendored upstream subtree` → `as a flat vendored tree prior to Sprint 2`.
   - **Line 47**: change `"Upstream video generation engine (vendored, DO NOT MODIFY)."` → `"Upstream video generation engine (vendored, do-not-edit-in-place)."` (lowercase — case-sensitive `rg` will not match).
   - **Line 103**: change `to remove stale vendored-subtree wording` → `to remove pre-Sprint-2 vendored-tree wording`.
   - **Lines 114-115 (the literal `rg` command block)**: replace the entire code block with the following prose, which contains neither the narrow pattern's two keywords on any line nor the uppercase non-modification notice:
     ```
     Doc-sweep gate: run the two regex sweeps specified in Sprint 2's brief
     (gate C). Both must return zero hits across `docs/`, `README.md`, and
     `STRUCTURE.md`. Regex literals are intentionally omitted here to avoid
     self-matching the gate commands.
     ```
   - **Self-verify the rewritten file** with `rg "vendored.*subtree" docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` and `rg 'DO NOT MODIFY' docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` — both must return zero hits before moving on.
7. **Track `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` in git** as part of Sprint 2 (currently untracked). Stage it alongside the other doc updates so the gate passes in the post-commit state.

### Step 9: Doc-sweep gate — ZERO hits required (`reigh-worker/docs/`, `reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Narrow sweep** — must return exactly zero lines:
   ```bash
   rg "vendored.*subtree" docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md
   ```
2. **Broader sweep** — must return exactly zero lines:
   ```bash
   rg -l 'Wan2GP' docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md | xargs -I{} rg -H -n 'DO NOT MODIFY' {}
   ```
3. **Zero exceptions**. If either sweep returns any line, Sprint 2 is not done. Fix the matching line and re-run.

## Phase 4: Land

### Step 10: Commit the submodule cutover + doc updates (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Stage**: `.gitmodules`, `Wan2GP` (the gitlink), all deletions under `Wan2GP/`, `README.md`, `STRUCTURE.md`, `docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`.
2. **Commit** with a message like `chore: replace vendored Wan2GP tree with submodule at reigh-sprint-2`. Reference the baseline tag in the body.
3. **Do not push without explicit user confirmation** — the submodule commit permanently alters the repo shape.

## Execution Order

1. Preflight + baseline tag + ltx2 manifest capture (Step 1) — rollback anchor and byte-preservation seed.
2. Build the fork branch offline in a scratch clone (Steps 2–4). Pause for user confirmation before the push in Step 4.7.
3. Cut reigh-worker over to the submodule (Step 5), then gate on the cheap filesystem smoke (Step 6.1).
4. Real unmocked bridge smoke (Step 6.2) and ltx2 manifest diff (Step 6.3).
5. Travel suite + bridge-contract tests (Step 7).
6. Doc rewrites (Step 8) with per-file self-verification; zero-hit doc-sweep gate (Step 9).
7. Commit locally (Step 10). Do not push.

## Validation Order

1. Cheapest: strict path-contract smoke (Step 6.1).
2. Fast: ltx2 manifest diff (Step 6.3), real bridge smoke (Step 6.2), per-file doc-rewrite self-verification (Step 8.6), doc-sweep regex gates (Step 9).
3. Slowest: 6-file travel suite + bridge-contract pytest (Step 7).

Rollback if ANY hard gate fails (path-contract smoke, bridge-import smoke, manifest diff, 6-file suite, bridge-contract tests, or doc-sweep gate):
```bash
git submodule deinit Wan2GP && git reset --hard wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline
```
The banodoco `reigh-sprint-2` branch stays in place after rollback (no force-delete) so retry does not have to rebuild it.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      }
    ]

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 2 (Revised v4)

## Overview

Sprint 2 replaces `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` (a 1400-file flat-copied tree, currently tracked in reigh-worker git) with a git submodule mounted at the same path, pointing at an integration branch on `banodoco/Wan2GP`. Four concrete outputs:

1. Submodule cutover that preserves the SD-001 path contract. Keeping `Wan2GP/` on `sys.path` resolves **all** bare-module imports across the vendored runtime — the three named in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py:76-88` (`models.qwen.qwen_handler`, `shared.utils.loras_mutipliers`, `models.qwen.qwen_main`) plus every other tree-wide bare caller (`Wan2GP/wgp.py`, `Wan2GP/models/wan/any2video.py`, `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/*.py`, preprocessors, plugins). Same seam covers all of them.
2. `shared/` + `postprocessing/` resync honoring `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` bucket decisions.
3. `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py` introduced from upstream HEAD plus its companion preset `Wan2GP/profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json` (both bucket-(a) per CSV rows 110 and 195). SD-005 off-by-default is satisfied because no `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2*.json` is modified to reference `res_2s` — preset is opt-in only. `Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` aligned to upstream HEAD.
4. Stale-doc wording removed from `README.md:39`, `STRUCTURE.md:22`, `STRUCTURE.md:70`, `docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md:202`, and from `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` wherever the hard `rg` gate patterns match. Replacement text must itself pass the gates (no self-matching).

**LTX2 non-negotiable clarified**: The rule applies to reigh-worker-visible bytes in `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/`. Verification uses a per-file content manifest captured before cutover and compared after cutover. Works across the submodule boundary. See Steps 1.3 and 6.3.

**Doc-sweep gate clarified**: Both `rg` commands from the idea must return **zero hits**, full stop. Any replacement text written into `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` must contain neither `vendored` co-occurring with `subtree` on any line, nor the uppercase non-modification notice anywhere.

**Fork-repo path namespace**: CSV paths are written with the `Wan2GP/` prefix (e.g., `Wan2GP/shared/api.py`). Fork-repo paths (relative to `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/` root) are the CSV path with exactly that prefix stripped — **no re-prefix**. For CSV row `Wan2GP/shared/api.py` the fork-repo path is `shared/api.py`, used directly as `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- shared/api.py`. This rule applies uniformly to all fork-repo commands in Step 4.

## Phase 0: Preflight

### Step 1: Record baseline tag and capture pre-cutover ltx2 manifest (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** working tree is clean (expected untracked only: `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`, `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`, `scripts/live_test/`). Confirm HEAD is current `main`.
2. **Create annotated tag** `wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline` on reigh-worker HEAD: `git tag -a wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline -m "Sprint 2 baseline"`. Do NOT push.
3. **Capture pre-cutover ltx2 per-file content fingerprint** (works identically pre- and post-cutover; survives the submodule boundary):
   ```bash
   (cd Wan2GP && find models/ltx2 -type f -not -name 'res2s.py' | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum) > /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.manifest
   ```
   The `-not -name 'res2s.py'` filter is a no-op pre-cutover (file doesn't exist yet) but ensures the post-cutover manifest (which will contain `res2s.py`) is comparable apples-to-apples.
4. **Verify fork access**: confirm `banodoco/Wan2GP` is reachable, capture `main` tip SHA + upstream `deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP` HEAD SHA.

## Phase 1: Construct the `reigh-sprint-2` fork branch

### Step 2: Clone the banodoco fork into a scratch directory (`/tmp/wan2gp-fork/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Clone**: `git clone https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git /tmp/wan2gp-fork` and `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork fetch origin main`.
2. **Branch from `main`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout -b reigh-sprint-2 origin/main`. Starting point = upstream HEAD (Sprint 1 state).
3. **Confirm** tip SHA matches Sprint 1's fork cut.

### Step 3: Mirror banodoco-drift onto `reigh-sprint-2` (byte-preserving carry commit)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Intent**: the fork branch's tree must equal `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` as-of baseline for every path — preserves all banodoco drift including the `models/ltx2/` hot zone. Bytes only, no transformation.
2. **Non-negotiable reconciliation**: the fork's carry commit writes bytes for `models/ltx2/*` on the fork, but those bytes are byte-identical to the pre-Sprint-2 reigh-worker baseline. The Sprint 2 no-touch rule governs reigh-worker bytes; Step 6.3 proves the reigh-worker-visible ltx2 tree is unchanged.
3. **Rsync copy**: from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/Wan2GP/` into `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/`, excluding `.git`, `__pycache__`, and anything already gitignored. Preserve the fork's existing `.gitignore` from upstream (don't overwrite).
4. **Stage + inspect**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork add -A` then `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork status`. Spot-check the staged delta matches expected banodoco drift: ~22 `models/ltx2/` file diffs, banodoco-only files in `defaults/` / `loras_qwen/` / `plugins/`, etc.
5. **Commit**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork commit -m "banodoco carry: mirror reigh-worker/Wan2GP baseline onto fork"`.

### Step 4: Apply Sprint 2 resync delta on top of the carry commit
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`shared/` triage application** — for every row in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` whose CSV path starts with `Wan2GP/shared/`, compute the fork-repo path by stripping exactly the `Wan2GP/` prefix (nothing else). The stripped result IS the complete fork-repo path — do NOT re-prefix with `shared/`. Worked examples:
   - CSV `Wan2GP/shared/api.py` → fork-repo path `shared/api.py`
   - CSV `Wan2GP/shared/utils/audio_video.py` → fork-repo path `shared/utils/audio_video.py`
   - CSV `Wan2GP/shared/gradio/gallery.py` → fork-repo path `shared/gradio/gallery.py`

   Then apply per bucket using the fork-repo path directly:
   - **Bucket (a) `accept-upstream`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- <fork-repo-path>`. Example: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- shared/api.py`.
   - **Bucket (b) `upstreamable`**: leave as carry commit (banodoco content preserved). No command.
   - **Bucket (c) `banodoco-fork-or-banodoco-patch`**: leave as carry commit. No command.
   - **Bucket (d) `cruft`**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork rm <fork-repo-path>`. (None expected in `shared/`; verify by grepping CSV for `shared/.*,cruft,`.)
2. **`postprocessing/` triage application**: the CSV has zero `Wan2GP/postprocessing/` rows, which means Sprint 1 triage confirmed no drift vs upstream. Verify: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork diff origin/main -- postprocessing/` must be empty. **If drift is found, HALT Sprint 2** and extend the triage CSV with proper bucket classifications before proceeding. Do NOT auto-classify.
3. **`shared/utils/self_refiner.py` alignment**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- shared/utils/self_refiner.py`. Per plan §2c, drift-only resync to upstream HEAD.
4. **`models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py` introduction**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`. CSV row 110 — bucket (a), upstream-only addition. Net-new file; no existing ltx2 file modified.
5. **`profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json` introduction**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork checkout origin/main -- "profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json"`. CSV row 195 — bucket (a), upstream-only addition, paired companion to `res2s.py`. **SD-005 verification**: before committing, grep upstream's `defaults/ltx2*.json` for any `res_2s` references — expected: none. If a default config already references `res_2s`, **HALT** and re-plan (would violate SD-005).
6. **Commit the Sprint 2 delta**: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork commit -m "sprint-2: resync shared/ + postprocessing/, align self_refiner, introduce res2s + HQ preset"`. Verify with `git show --stat HEAD` that the diff is confined to fork-repo paths under `shared/` and `postprocessing/`, plus `models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py` and `profiles/ltx2_presets/HQ (Res2s).json`. All paths are relative to `/tmp/wan2gp-fork/` — no `Wan2GP/` prefix appears in the git output.
7. **Pause for user confirmation before pushing** `reigh-sprint-2` to `banodoco/Wan2GP` (visible-to-banodoco action). After confirmation: `git -C /tmp/wan2gp-fork push -u origin reigh-sprint-2`.
8. **Capture tip SHA** for the submodule pointer.

## Phase 2: Reigh-worker submodule cutover

### Step 5: Replace flat `Wan2GP/` tree with submodule (`reigh-worker/.gitmodules`, `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Remove tracked flat tree**: `git rm -r --cached Wan2GP/` (removes from index, leaves working copy). Then `rm -rf Wan2GP/` to remove the physical directory.
2. **Add submodule**: `git submodule add -b reigh-sprint-2 https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git Wan2GP`. Creates `.gitmodules`, populates `.git/modules/Wan2GP/`, checks out branch tip into `Wan2GP/`.
3. **Pin to the specific SHA** from Step 4.8: `git -C Wan2GP checkout <sha>` then `git add Wan2GP .gitmodules`.
4. **Verify `.gitmodules`**:
   ```
   [submodule "Wan2GP"]
       path = Wan2GP
       url = https://github.com/banodoco/Wan2GP.git
       branch = reigh-sprint-2
   ```
5. **Verify submodule health**: `git submodule status` shows `Wan2GP` at the pinned SHA with no `+`/`-` prefix.
6. **Broader caller note**: no changes required to `source/core/runtime_paths.py` — absolute `Wan2GP/` on `sys.path` continues to satisfy every bare-import caller across the vendored tree (the 3 named in `vendor_imports.py:76-88` plus all internal callers in `Wan2GP/wgp.py`, `Wan2GP/models/wan/*`, `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/*`, preprocessors, plugins). The 26+ dotted `Wan2GP.*` callers remain supported because the repo root is also on `sys.path`.
7. **Do not commit yet** — run validation gates first.

### Step 6: Validate path-contract, bridge imports, and ltx2 byte preservation (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Strict path-contract one-liner** (filesystem-only per plan §2i; safe in any Python env):
   ```bash
   python3 -c "import os, sys; from source.core.runtime_paths import ensure_wan2gp_on_path, get_wan2gp_path; ensure_wan2gp_on_path(); assert os.path.abspath(str(get_wan2gp_path())) in [os.path.abspath(p) for p in sys.path]; assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_handler.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py'); print('path-contract smoke OK')"
   ```
   Must exit 0 with `path-contract smoke OK`.
2. **Real unmocked bridge-import smoke** (runs in the worker venv where SciPy is available):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 python -c "from source.runtime.wgp_ports.vendor_imports import get_qwen_family_handler, get_shared_lora_utils_module, get_qwen_main_module; get_qwen_family_handler(); get_shared_lora_utils_module(); get_qwen_main_module(); print('bridge imports OK')"
   ```
   Real filesystem-backed exercise of the three bare-module callers. Direct coverage of the bridge seam.
3. **LTX2 byte-preservation check** using the manifest captured in Step 1.3:
   ```bash
   (cd Wan2GP && find models/ltx2 -type f -not -name 'res2s.py' | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum) > /tmp/ltx2_post_cutover.manifest
   diff /tmp/ltx2_pre_cutover.manifest /tmp/ltx2_post_cutover.manifest
   ```
   `diff` must exit 0 with empty output — proves every pre-existing file under `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` has byte-identical content post-cutover. Additionally verify `res2s.py` exists: `test -f Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/res2s.py`.

### Step 7: Run the 6-file travel regression suite + bridge-contract regressions (`tests/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **6-file travel suite** (idea gate B):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py
   ```
   All green. This is the Sprint 2 behavior-preservation regression baseline. It does not directly exercise `vendor_imports` or `wgp_bridge`; Step 6.2 covers the bridge seam directly.
2. **Supplementary bridge-contract regressions** (covers the API surface of the bridge):
   ```bash
   uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_bridge_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_registry_readonly_contracts.py
   ```
   Must be all green. Even though these tests partially monkeypatch `ensure_wan2gp_on_path`, running them post-cutover verifies the bridge API hasn't regressed at the interface level; Step 6.2 separately verifies the unmocked filesystem mount.
3. **On failure**: inspect whether a resynced `shared/` file touches travel-path code. If yes, reclassify the triggering file to bucket (c) on the fork branch, amend Step 4.1, re-push, re-pin. Do NOT silently widen the resync.

## Phase 3: Doc updates

### Step 8: Rewrite stale vendored wording (`reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`README.md:39`** — replace "vendored upstream input ... subtree" wording with submodule-accurate language describing pointer bumps and mirroring upstream requirement changes into root worker metadata before regenerating `uv.lock`.
2. **`README.md:103`** (Powered By section, if present) — correct "the `Wan2GP/` directory contains the upstream engine" to note the submodule pinned to `banodoco/Wan2GP`.
3. **`STRUCTURE.md:22`** — replace `vendored upstream. Dependency drift ...` with submodule-accurate language describing pointer bumps rather than subtree edits.
4. **`STRUCTURE.md:70`** — replace `Upstream video generation engine (vendored, DO NOT MODIFY)` with `Git submodule pointing at banodoco/Wan2GP; edit via fork PR + pointer bump, not in-place`.
5. **`docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md:202`** — replace `still a vendored upstream subtree` with submodule language; preserve the paragraph's existing guidance about mirroring requirements into root worker metadata.
6. **`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`** — required rewrites so both hard `rg` gates pass:
   - **Line 45**: change `as a vendored upstream subtree` → `as a flat vendored tree prior to Sprint 2`.
   - **Line 47**: change `"Upstream video generation engine (vendored, DO NOT MODIFY)."` → `"Upstream video generation engine (vendored, do-not-edit-in-place)."` (lowercase — case-sensitive `rg` will not match).
   - **Line 103**: change `to remove stale vendored-subtree wording` → `to remove pre-Sprint-2 vendored-tree wording`.
   - **Lines 114-115 (the literal `rg` command block)**: replace with the following prose:
     ```
     Doc-sweep gate: run the two regex sweeps specified in Sprint 2's brief
     (gate C). Both must return zero hits across `docs/`, `README.md`, and
     `STRUCTURE.md`. Regex literals are intentionally omitted here to avoid
     self-matching the gate commands.
     ```
   - **Self-verify the rewritten file**: `rg "vendored.*subtree" docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` and `rg 'DO NOT MODIFY' docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` — both must return zero hits before moving on.
7. **Track `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` in git** as part of Sprint 2 (currently untracked). Stage it alongside the other doc updates so the gate passes in the post-commit state.

### Step 9: Doc-sweep gate — ZERO hits required (`reigh-worker/docs/`, `reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Narrow sweep** — must return exactly zero lines:
   ```bash
   rg "vendored.*subtree" docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md
   ```
2. **Broader sweep** — must return exactly zero lines:
   ```bash
   rg -l 'Wan2GP' docs/ README.md STRUCTURE.md | xargs -I{} rg -H -n 'DO NOT MODIFY' {}
   ```
3. **Zero exceptions**. If either sweep returns any line, Sprint 2 is not done. Fix the matching line and re-run.

## Phase 4: Land

### Step 10: Commit the submodule cutover + doc updates (`reigh-worker/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Stage**: `.gitmodules`, `Wan2GP` (the gitlink), all deletions under `Wan2GP/`, `README.md`, `STRUCTURE.md`, `docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`.
2. **Commit** with a message like `chore: replace vendored Wan2GP tree with submodule at reigh-sprint-2`. Reference the baseline tag in the body.
3. **Do not push without explicit user confirmation** — the submodule commit permanently alters the repo shape.

## Execution Order

1. Preflight + baseline tag + ltx2 manifest capture (Step 1) — rollback anchor and byte-preservation seed.
2. Build the fork branch offline in a scratch clone (Steps 2–4). Pause for user confirmation before the push in Step 4.7.
3. Cut reigh-worker over to the submodule (Step 5), then gate on the cheap filesystem smoke (Step 6.1).
4. Real unmocked bridge smoke (Step 6.2) and ltx2 manifest diff (Step 6.3).
5. Travel suite + bridge-contract tests (Step 7).
6. Doc rewrites (Step 8) with per-file self-verification; zero-hit doc-sweep gate (Step 9).
7. Commit locally (Step 10). Do not push.

## Validation Order

1. Cheapest: strict path-contract smoke (Step 6.1).
2. Fast: ltx2 manifest diff (Step 6.3), real bridge smoke (Step 6.2), per-file doc-rewrite self-verification (Step 8.6), doc-sweep regex gates (Step 9).
3. Slowest: 6-file travel suite + bridge-contract pytest (Step 7).

Rollback if ANY hard gate fails:
```bash
git submodule deinit Wan2GP && git reset --hard wan2gp-mig-sprint-2-baseline
```
The banodoco `reigh-sprint-2` branch stays in place after rollback (no force-delete).

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 2,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

> AGENT

Looking at iteration 4, the convergence trajectory has **reversed** for the first time: 23.5 → 15.5 → 9.0 → **10.0**. Plan delta has collapsed to 7.9% (v4 was almost entirely the targeted Step 4.1 fix). V4 correctly resolved all 5 path-mapping flags I identified last round — the Step 4.1 rewrite is clean with worked examples.

But the critic surfaced a **new cluster of 6 flags** all pointing at the same underlying concern: `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` §4 Sprint 2's verification matrix lists an "LTX-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline" as a hard gate, but the execution plan keeps it only as criterion #22 (info-level, `verify_physical_device`), not as a runnable step in Section 6/7.

**What's happening in the loop:**

1. **The concern is factually real**: Deliverable 3 explicitly modifies `shared/utils/self_refiner.py` (imported by `Wan2GP/wgp.py`, `models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/*.py`, `models/wan/any2video.py` per critic search). A behavior-preservation resync with zero runtime verification is a real gap, and the plan doc names a specific smoke to fill it.

2. **But the critic is also moving goalposts**: The self_refiner runnable-smoke gap existed identically in v1, v2, v3, v4 — this is the first iteration where any critic flagged it. That's legitimate catch-up discovery, but it also means we're finding latent issues rather than fixing newly-introduced ones.

3. **My prior commitment**: I said in iteration 3 "iteration 4 is the last free pass — if v4 fixes Step 4.1 but introduces a new small defect elsewhere, ESCALATE becomes the right call." V4 did not strictly *introduce* a new defect (self_refiner was always info-level), but the score did reverse, which is the signal that loop has stopped converging.

**Why not ITERATE**: One more round adds a runnable `self_refiner` smoke, but the critic pattern suggests another latent concern will surface next round. 4 iterations is past the standard-robustness budget. The score reversal is the clearest signal that continued iteration isn't productive.

**Why not PROCEED**: I can construct an `accept_tradeoff` rationale (the idea brief specifies three hard gates A/B/C, self_refiner smoke was in the plan doc but NOT promoted to a hard gate in the idea, and criterion #22 preserves it). But that's a substantive product call that should come from the user, not the gatekeeper — it's asking whether to expand Sprint 2's hard-gate scope beyond what the idea names.

**Why not TIEBREAKER**: Formal criteria not met. `addressed_then_reopened_count=0` across all groups; member-flag counts per group don't reach 2 across 2 iterations for the substantive groups (FG-004 is 1 flag across 4 iterations). FG-010 has 4 member flags but they're criterion-boilerplate (`subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device`) warnings, not architectural tensions.

**Why ESCALATE**: The user needs to decide one of:
- (a) **Accept as-is**: the three idea-named gates are sufficient; self_refiner stays as info-level criterion #22; executor optionally runs self_refiner smoke per the plan doc during implementation.
- (b) **Expand scope**: formally promote self_refiner behavioral smoke to a runnable hard gate (requires either a specific unmocked import smoke or a functional test with pre/post baseline).
- (c) **Override**: direct PROCEED and accept the trade-off explicitly.

This is a product/scope decision, not a plan-quality fix the writer can perform without user direction on which specific self_refiner smoke to run (a bare `from shared.utils.self_refiner import ...` smoke? A functional call? A golden-output comparison? Each has different cost and coverage).
